Our estimates indicate that the width of $T_{c\bar{s}1}^{a}(2470)$ is roughly three orders of magnitude larger than that of $T_{c\bar{s}1}^{f}(2460)$, while the widths of $T_{\bar{c}\bar{s}1}^{a/f}(2470)$ are of the same orders as that of $T_{c\bar{s}1}^{f}(2460)$. The present study may provide valuable clues for the experimental search for these molecular candidates.

</details>


### [65] [Flavor--Kinetic Entanglement Production from Decay and Scattering at Finite Density](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07995)
*Zekun Li,Jia Liu,Xiao-Ping Wang,Jing-Jun Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the scattering-entanglement relationship to finite-density environments, demonstrating a connection between collision probabilities and entanglement entropy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the behavior of systems at finite density through the lens of flavor-kinetic entanglement.

Method: By analyzing the flavor-kinetic bipartition of the Hilbert space and tracing over kinematic degrees of freedom, the authors link branch-changing probabilities to flavor-kinetic entanglement entropy.

Result: The study reveals that the bath-averaged flavor-kinetic entanglement entropy can serve as an effective diagnostic for phase transitions, indicating finite discontinuities and nonanalytic behavior corresponding to first-order and continuous transitions, respectively.

Conclusion: The proposed framework presents a novel characterization of thermal phase structures that differs from conventional thermodynamic parameters.

Abstract: We extend the scattering-entanglement dictionary to finite-density environments by investigating the flavor--kinetic bipartition of the Hilbert space. We show that tracing over kinematic degrees of freedom maps the total branch-changing transition probability directly onto the leading flavor--kinetic linear entanglement entropy. At finite density, the vacuum branch-changing probability is replaced by an occupation-weighted collision probability, built from the same directed reaction-density kernel that enters the integrated Boltzmann equation. The resulting observable is the bath-averaged flavor--kinetic entanglement entropy of a pair sampled from the medium. As a proof of principle, this framework is applied to an $O(N)$ singlet-scalar extended model to probe thermal phase transitions. In the examples studied, the resulting entanglement entropy serves as a collision-based phase-transition-type diagnostic, exhibiting a finite discontinuity across a first-order phase transition and a nonanalytic temperature derivative for continuous transitions. These examples suggest a novel way to characterize thermal phase structures, distinct from traditional thermodynamic order parameters.

</details>


### [66] [Topological analysis of scale-invariant spatial fluctuations in ultrarelativistic heavy-ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08259)
*Salman Khurshid Malik,Ramni Gupta,Fakhar Ul Haider,Balwan Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a novel two-stage topological machine learning approach to analyze QGP-to-hadronic matter transitions in heavy-ion collisions, overcoming challenges in signal extraction through traditional methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Identifying the QGP-to-hadronic matter phase transition and QCD critical point is crucial in heavy-ion collision studies, but traditional methods struggle due to weak critical fluctuation signals amidst dominant non-critical backgrounds.

Method: A two-stage topological machine learning framework is employed, combining Topological Data Analysis (TDA) with deep learning to analyze two-dimensional angular phase space data, using Delaunay-based filtrations and classifications via TopoPointNet and Boosted Decision Trees.

Result: The methodology effectively restores power-law scaling in the normalized factorial moments, allowing for accurate measurement of the intermittency index in phase space and revealing critical signals previously obscured by background noise.

Conclusion: This study establishes topological machine learning as a promising tool for probing the QCD critical point and enhances understanding of the phase structure in heavy-ion collision dynamics.

Abstract: The QGP-to-hadronic matter phase transition and QCD critical point in heavy-ion collisions can be identified by studying spatial fluctuations among final-state particles using intermittency analysis. First CMC-based intermittency analysis in the two-dimensional angular ($η$, $\varphi$) phase space, using EPOS as the background model is presented. Critical fluctuation signals are extremely weak, constituting only a few percent of the total event sample and are severely diluted by the overwhelming non-critical background, rendering traditional intermittency analyses insufficient for reliable signal extraction. To extract the weak critical signal, we employ a two-stage topological machine learning framework combining Topological Data Analysis (TDA) with deep learning. In the first stage, particle events are represented as two-dimensional point clouds and a Delaunay-based sub-level set filtration is constructed to extract Betti curves as multiscale topological invariants, corrected for multiplicity bias via azimuthal randomisation and classified by two complementary architectures, a TopoPointNet (TPN) and Boosted Decision Trees (BDT). Since event-level classification alone is insufficient to restore the critical scaling, a second stage applies a particle-level density filter, explicitly stripping away the diffuse thermal background and isolating the densely packed critical clusters. The two stage pipeline successfully restores the power-law scaling of the normalized factorial moments, enabling accurate recovery of the intermittency index in ($η$, $\varphi$) space and establishing topological machine learning as a robust data driven tool for probing the QCD critical point and the phase structure of strongly interacting matter in heavy-ion collisions at LHC energies.

</details>


### [67] [Hadronic photon correction to $γ^{\ast} γ\to f_{2}(1270)$ at next-to-leading order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08054)
*Bing-Xin Liu,Shen-Qiang Ren*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper computes hadronic photon corrections to the $γ^*γ	o f_2(1270)$ transition form factors using light-cone sum rules and perturbative methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to enhance understanding of transition form factors related to photonic interactions in hadronic physics.

Method: The authors employ light-cone sum rules, establish a factorization formula at next-to-leading order, extract hard matching coefficients, and resummate large logarithms using two-loop evolution equations.

Result: They derive updated theoretical predictions for the helicity form factors $T_0(Q^2)$, $T_1(Q^2)$, and $T_2(Q^2)$, along with an estimate of theoretical uncertainties.

Conclusion: This work improves theoretical predictions for hadronic processes involving photon interactions, which can be crucial for experimental verification.

Abstract: Within the framework of light-cone sum rules, we calculate the hadronic photon corrections to the $γ^*γ\to f_2(1270)$ transition form factors induced by the leading-twist photon distribution amplitude of the real photon. We establish the factorization formula for the vacuum-to-photon correlation function at next-to-leading order in $α_s$, and extract the perturbative hard matching coefficients by applying the method of regions. The parametrically large logarithms appearing in the hard functions are resummed to next-to-leading logarithmic accuracy by solving the two-loop evolution equation for the corresponding light-ray tensor operator. Combining the resulting light-cone sum rules with the known leading-power contributions from QCD collinear factorization, we provide updated theoretical predictions for the three helicity form factors $T_0(Q^2)$, $T_1(Q^2)$ and $T_2(Q^2)$, including an estimate of the theoretical uncertainties.

</details>


### [68] [QCD Sum Rule Analysis of Triply Heavy $(Q\bar{Q})(Q\bar{q})$ Tetraquark States with $J^P=0^{\pm}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08385)
*Wen-Shuai Zhang,Chun-Gui Duan,Zhi-Hui Guo,Liang Tang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study investigates tetraquark states in QCD sum rules, identifying four charmonium states and decay patterns, predicting narrow states for some and suggesting experimental search strategies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding tetraquark states can provide insights into their mass spectra and decay behaviors, contributing to the broader knowledge of exotic hadrons in QCD.

Method: The authors employ QCD sum rules, analyzing two-point correlation functions to extract mass spectra and decay patterns of tetraquark states with specified quantum numbers.

Result: Identified four tetraquark states in the $(car{c})(car{q})$ system and determined mass ranges for $(bar{b})(bar{q})$ states, detailing possible decay modes.

Conclusion: Tetraquark states like $T_{3c,0}(4760)$ are predicted to be narrow and promising for experimental searches, while others are expected to have significant decay widths.

Abstract: Within the framework of QCD sum rules, we systematically investigate the mass spectra and possible decay patterns of the $(c\bar{c})(c\bar{q})$ and $(b\bar{b})(b\bar{q})$ tetraquark states with quantum numbers $J^{P}=0^{\pm}$. Based on two distinct color configurations, $[8_c]_{Q\bar{Q}} \otimes [8_c]_{Q\bar{q}}$ and $[1_c]_{Q\bar{Q}} \otimes [1_c]_{Q\bar{q}}$, we construct 18 interpolating currents for these states, and obtain stable sum rules for a subset of them. By calculating the corresponding two-point correlation functions, we extract their mass spectra. For the $(c\bar{c})(c\bar{q})$ system, we identify four possible tetraquark states: two with $J^{P}=0^+$, namely $T_{3c,0}(4760)$ and $T_{3c,0}(5000)$, and two with $J^{P}=0^-$, denoted as $T_{3c,0}(5040)$ and $T_{3c,0}(5370)$. For the $(b\bar{b})(b\bar{q})$ system, the extracted masses are found to lie in the ranges $13.72$--$14.02$ GeV for the $J^{P}=0^+$ states and $13.90$--$14.22$ GeV for the $J^{P}=0^-$ states. We further analyze their possible decay modes. Our results indicate that $T_{3c,0}(5000)$, $T_{3c,0}(5040)$, and $T_{3c,0}(5370)$ can decay into a charmonium state and a charmed meson, and are therefore expected to have appreciable decay widths. By contrast, $T_{3c,0}(4760)$ and all predicted $(b\bar{b})(b\bar{q})$ tetraquark states are expected to be relatively narrow, since the corresponding two-body strong decays via the fall-apart mechanism are kinematically forbidden. Therefore, $T_{3c,0}(4760)$ and all predicted $(b\bar{b})(b\bar{q})$ tetraquark states are promising candidates for experimental searches in final states containing a $D$ or a $\bar{B}$ meson, accompanied by light hadrons or a photon.

</details>


### [69] [Neutrino Mass and its Impact on Gravitational Waves from Domain Wall Collision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08084)
*Victoria Puyam,Mrinal Kumar Das*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study proposes two models using $A_{4} 	imes Z_{4}$ symmetry to analyze neutrino masses, mixings, and gravitational-wave spectra from domain-wall annihilation, achieving consistent results with current neutrino data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore neutrino masses and mixings while examining the gravitational-wave spectrum from domain-wall annihilation.

Method: Constructed two models: one with flavon's vacuum expectation value derived from self-interacting potential; the second with a new vacuum expectation value from both self-interaction and mixed terms, incorporating flavon mixing to address vacua degeneracy.

Result: Both models align well with existing neutrino oscillation data, presenting varying mixing patterns and successfully lifting degeneracy through flavon mixing terms.

Conclusion: The predicted gravitational-wave spectrum could be observable by future experiments if flavons achieve a vacuum expectation of $10^4$ TeV.

Abstract: The $A_{4} \times Z_{4}$ symmetry models are constructed to study neutrino masses and mixings, as well as the gravitational-wave spectrum from domain-wall annihilation. The first neutrino mass model is constructed with the flavon's vacuum expectation value and alignment obtained from the self-interacting potential terms, while the second model uses a new vacuum expectation value arising from a potential containing both self-interaction and mixed terms. The resulting neutrino mixing patterns for both models are in good agreement with current neutrino oscillation data with different mixing values. Further, the flavon mixing terms lift the vacua degeneracy that often shows in the spontaneous symmetry breaking of the discrete symmetry. These mixing terms and the modified neutrino mass matrix of the second model are considered to produce the necessary bias for analysing the gravitational waves spectrum. The spectrum predicted by the model could be detected by current and near-future experiments when the flavons have the vacuum expectation value of $10^4$ TeV.

</details>


### [70] [Near-threshold resonances in $e^+e^-$ annihilation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08769)
*S. G. Salnikov,A. E. Bondar,A. I. Milstein*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper reviews near-threshold resonances in hadron pair production, highlighting their effects on reaction cross sections due to final-state interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the implications of near-threshold resonances on hadron pair production and cross-section behavior.

Method: A comprehensive review of experimental results and theoretical explanations regarding final-state interactions in hadron pair production processes.

Result: Final-state interactions are shown to explain the energy dependence of cross sections in various experiments involving near-threshold resonances.

Conclusion: Studying near-threshold resonances provides insights into hadron interactions at large distances.

Abstract: Near-threshold resonances have been discovered in many hadron pair production processes, resulting in a significant increase in reaction cross sections at low relative velocities of the produced particles. The natural cause of such resonances is the interaction between slow hadrons in the final state. Our review demonstrates that, in virtually all known cases, final-state interactions successfully explain the results of numerous experiments demonstrating a nontrivial energy dependence of cross sections for processes near reaction thresholds. A comprehensive study of near-threshold resonances in various processes can provide new information about the interaction between hadrons at large distances.

</details>


### [71] [Probing Memory-Burdened Primordial Black Holes with High-Energy Neutrinos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08144)
*Arian Moradi Asl,Sandhya Choubey,Andreas Lund*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes the survival and detection of lighter primordial black holes (PBHs) under the memory-burden effect, assessing current and future observational constraints and sensitivities across different mass distribution models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how memory-burdened primordial black holes can evade standard evaporation limits and contribute to high-energy neutrino emissions, offering new avenues for detection.

Method: Constraints from existing IceCube data and projections for future detectors (IceCube-Gen2 and GRAND200k) were employed to evaluate the impact of different mass function models on signal detection.

Result: Log-normal distributed PBHs can be more constrained than monochromatic ones due to their low-mass tails. Current IceCube data provide limits on lower memory burden parameters, with future detectors promising enhanced sensitivity for higher values.

Conclusion: Future experiments could potentially identify a positive PBH signal, with clearer distinctions between mass distribution types, particularly favoring log-normal over monochromatic models.

Abstract: The memory-burden effect can suppress the late-time evaporation of primordial black holes (PBHs), allowing those below the standard Hawking evaporation threshold to survive until the present epoch. These lighter PBHs emit high and ultra-high-energy neutrinos, opening the tentalizing possibility of discovery via neutrino telescopes. We study the constraints on memory-burdened PBHs from current IceCube HESE, MESE, and EHE data; and forecast the sensitivity reach of IceCube-Gen2 radio and GRAND200k. In particular, we study how this signal depends on whether the surviving population is described by a log-normal mass function or by a monochromatic one. We find that log-normal distributed populations can be more strongly constrained than monochromatic populations with the same median mass primarily because their low-mass tails enhance the high-energy neutrino flux. We show that current IceCube data provide the leading limits for a lower memory burden parameter $k$, whereas the projected radio detectors become substantially more sensitive for higher values of $k$. We also study a scenario where future experiments would see a positive signal coming from PBHs. We consider a representative 30-event signal in IceCube-Gen2 and GRAND200k and study how well one could distinguish the two mass-function hypotheses. We find that it is easier to disfavor the monochromatic distribution when the log-normal distribution is assumed to be true. Finally, we study how well the parameters of the memory-burdened PBHs can be estimated in these future experiments.

</details>


### [72] [The Living Guide of Machine Learning for Particle Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09531)
*Claudius Krause,Ramon Winterhalder,Matthew Feickert,Benjamin Nachman*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The Living Review of Machine Learning for Particle Physics is transitioning to the HEP-ML Living Guide to better serve the maturing field, focusing on curated and annotated references instead of a comprehensive bibliography.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The rapid growth of literature in machine learning applications for particle physics created a need for a structured and continuously updated reference that could not be sustained by the original model.

Method: The Living Review will be frozen as an archival record until June 2026, while the new HEP-ML Living Guide will provide curated and annotated references to foundational and representative works.

Result: The shift from a comprehensive bibliography to a curated guide addresses the challenges posed by the expanding literature in the field and aims to aid researchers in navigation and understanding.

Conclusion: This change reflects the evolution of the HEP-ML community and the need for resources that adapt to a diversifying field.

Abstract: We started the Living Review of Machine Learning for Particle Physics (HEP-ML Living Review) in 2020 as a community-maintained, near-comprehensive bibliography of machine learning in particle physics. The field was then growing faster than any single researcher could follow, finding the relevant papers was hard, and a structured, continuously updated reference paid off immediately. Since then the literature has grown by more than an order of magnitude, the methods reach far beyond the classification and generation tasks of the early years, and the community has built its own ecosystem of topic-specific reviews, benchmark papers, and software frameworks. The original model no longer serves this field well, and we can no longer sustain it. We therefore change direction. We freeze the Living Review as an archival reference covering the literature up to 1 June 2026, where it remains a stable record of the first phase of HEP-ML. A new resource, the HEP-ML Living Guide, replaces it. It does not list everything. It curates, it annotates, and it points readers to foundational and representative work, so that researchers can find their way into a mature and rapidly diversifying field. In this article we explain why we make this change and how the new resource works.

</details>


### [73] [Open Path Geometric Phases in Three Flavor Neutrino Oscillations through Layered Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08179)
*N. Razzaghi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a gauge invariant approach to open path geometric phases for neutrino oscillations in variable matter, highlighting noncommutative flavor evolution and the impact of the Dirac CP phase on transition amplitudes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need to understand neutrino oscillations in nonuniform matter using a framework that accommodates open trajectories in parameter space, distinct from standard closed-loop geometric phase methods.

Method: A layered matter framework is introduced, where each layer has a constant density, allowing for the derivation of evolution operators as path-ordered products of dynamical factors and interface matrices. The approach ensures gauge invariance under local rephasings.

Result: The resulting geometric phase chains and their invariance under local transformations resolve gauge dependence issues, illustrating how different profiles lead to varied transition amplitudes and demonstrating the Dirac CP phase's influence on transport geometry.

Conclusion: The study provides a comprehensive understanding of geometric phases in neutrino oscillations, indicating that they are interlinked with transition probabilities through interference rather than being stand-alone observables.

Abstract: We present a gauge invariant formulation of open path geometric phases in the context of three flavor neutrino oscillations traversing nonuniform matter. In contrast to the standard Berry phase, which is intrinsically associated with closed adiabatic cycles, neutrino propagation from source to detector typically traces an open trajectory within the parameter space of matter dependent Hamiltonians. We derive this formalism within a layered matter framework, where each discrete layer is characterized by a constant density and a local matter eigenbasis. The total evolution operator is expressed as a path ordered product of dynamical propagation factors and interface matrices that bridge adjacent matter eigenbases. This construction naturally yields geometric phase chains composed of endpoint projections and interface overlaps. We demonstrate that these chains remain invariant under arbitrary local rephasings of the instantaneous matter eigenstates, thereby resolving the gauge dependence ambiguity typically associated with open path transport.
  The proposed formalism elucidates the noncommutative nature of flavor evolution in layered media, illustrating that profiles with identical column densities but distinct ordering can yield different transition amplitudes. Furthermore, we analyze the influence of the Dirac CP phase, showing that it deforms the geometry of matter eigenbasis transport without being equivalent to the geometric phase itself. In the continuous limit, finite interface overlaps converge to Berry connection factors, while endpoint projections remain indispensable for ensuring open path gauge invariance. We discuss the phenomenological implications of this structure, emphasizing that geometric phases are not independent observables but contribute to transition probabilities via interference among coherent propagation chains.

</details>


### [74] [Correlated low-energy constants in large-$N_c$ chiral perturbation theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08209)
*Pere Masjuan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper discusses the interrelationship between low-energy constants in the chiral Lagrangian for η and η' mesons, emphasizing correlated combinations rather than independent constants, and proposes a method to interpret these relationships in precision studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the implications of combined chiral and large-$N_c$ expansions in precision studies involving η and η' mesons and axion phenomenology.

Method: Analyzes the operator structure of the large-$N_c$ chiral Lagrangian to identify correlations among low-energy constants, exemplified by the determination of the pion decay constant.

Result: Shows that there are significant correlated directions among low-energy constants, especially revealing an anticorrelation between $F_0$ and $L_4$ that extends to NNLO with $C_{16}$.

Conclusion: Suggests a practical approach for interpreting and propagating phenomenological determinations of low-energy constants consistently within the combined chiral and large-$N_c$ framework.

Abstract: The combined chiral and large-$N_c$ expansion is increasingly employed in precision studies involving the $η$ and $η'$ mesons, including recent applications to low-energy axion phenomenology within U(3) chiral perturbation theory. We point out that the operator structure of the large-$N_c$ chiral Lagrangian naturally induces correlated directions among the low-energy constants $(F_0,L_4,C_{16})$ and $(F_0,L_6,C_{20})$, implying that phenomenological analyses determine correlated combinations of couplings rather than independent low-energy constants. Using the determination of the pion decay constant as an illustrative example, we reinterpret existing phenomenological and lattice determinations in terms of these correlated directions, showing that the well-known anticorrelation between $F_0$ and $L_4$ extends naturally to NNLO through $C_{16}$. These correlated directions lead to a practical prescription for interpreting and propagating phenomenological determinations of the low-energy constants consistently within the combined chiral and large-$N_c$ framework.

</details>


### [75] [Perturbative Dissipation in Minimal Warm Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08345)
*Daniel J. H. Chung,Nidhi Sudhir,Lian-Tao Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the dissipation modes in a warm inflation model with axion-inflaton coupling to a gluon thermal bath, emphasizing the role of Landau damping and plasmon decay in determining the friction coefficient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the perturbative dissipation modes in a minimal warm inflation framework and understand their impact on the friction coefficient in the axion-inflaton scenario.

Method: The analysis focuses on Landau damping of soft gluons and hard gluon plasmon decay to derive the friction coefficient, highlighting its dependence on the magnetic mass scale and slow roll suppression.

Result: The friction coefficient is linearly proportional to the coupling constant α, contrasting with the stronger suppression seen in Chern-Simons diffusion; local spontaneous CPT violation is also addressed.

Conclusion: Unsuppressed friction can arise from the time correlations of nonlocal variables, providing insights into derivative couplings in warm inflation.

Abstract: We present an analysis of the perturbative modes of dissipation in the minimal warm inflation model where an axion-inflaton is coupled to a gluon thermal bath through the axion-gauge coupling. The corresponding friction coefficient, which generically is governed by IR physics, for the processes we consider is dominated by Landau damping of soft space-like gluons and the plasmon decay of hard on shell gluons. Since both of these processes are IR dominated, the friction coefficient becomes sensitive to the non-perturbative magnetic mass scale $m_{g}^{-1} \sim (αT)^{-1}$ which in turn leads to the friction coefficient's overall linear proportionality to $α$, which is much less suppressed in $α$ powers compared to the well understood term from Chern-Simons (CS) diffusion $(\propto α^5)$. On the other hand, this contribution to the friction coefficient is suppressed due to slow roll. We show that this can be viewed as a suppression of local spontaneous CPT violation for derivative couplings in general and that time correlations of nonlocal variables which do not fall off at large time separations are what allow an unsuppressed friction coefficient.

</details>


### [76] [The $π/12$ model: trimaximal first-column lepton mixing with charged-lepton $μ$--$τ$ breaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08394)
*Vernon Barger*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study explores violations to the lepton mixing matrix's independent conditions, highlighting differences between two parameters, $$ and $$, based on current data and measurements. It presents a model aligned with these observations, predicting specific values for mixing angles and CP violation, and sets up a testable framework for future experimental results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the conditions imposed on the lepton mixing matrix and their violations, particularly in light of recent neutrino data.

Method: Parameterization of violations in the mixing matrix using $$ and $$, with a focus on data analysis, including measurements from the JUNO experiment and constraints from the $S_4$ symmetry approach.

Result: The analysis reveals an asymmetry in the violation parameters, with $$ close to zero and $$ significantly non-zero, indicating a preference for the $π/12$ model and specific predictions for mixing angles and CP phase, necessitating further experimental verification.

Conclusion: Predictions align with the current measurements but have discrepancies that can be tested with upcoming experiments, particularly with potential high-precision results from Hyper-Kamiokande.

Abstract: The first column of the lepton mixing matrix carries two independent magnitude conditions: the trimaximal norm $|U_{e1}|^2=2/3$ and the $μ$--$τ$ balance $|U_{\mu1}|=|U_{\tau1}|$. We parameterize their violation by $\varepsilon_1\equiv(3/2)|U_{e1}|^2-1$ and $\varepsilon_2\equiv3(|U_{\tau1}|^2-|U_{\mu1}|^2)$ and show that current data treat the two differently in central value: including the first JUNO measurement, $\varepsilon_1=+0.014\pm0.010$ is consistent with zero, while $\varepsilon_2=+0.29^{+0.07}_{-0.13}$ is large but disfavors zero at only $1.5σ$ on the global-fit profile likelihood, the driving phase being still poorly measured. This asymmetry selects the $π/12$ model. Its neutrino sector is TM$_1$ with the electron-row condition $|U_{e2}|/|U_{e3}|=2+\sqrt3$ that fixes $\sin^2θ_{13}=(2-\sqrt3)/12=0.02233$ and $\sin^2θ_{12}=0.31811$. A charged-lepton 2--3 rotation $R_{23}(θ_e,α)$ leaves the electron row exactly invariant and generates $\varepsilon_2=\sin2θ_e\cosα$. In an $S_4\times C_4\times C_3\times C_2$ realization, the rotation arises from a single additional flavon whose alignment forces $α=0$; a natural angle $θ_e\simeq2^\circ$ then reproduces the departure of $θ_{23}$ from maximal while pinning the CP phase to $δ=272^\circ\pm2^\circ$, near-maximal CP violation with $\varepsilon_2=-0.066$. The current $θ_{23}$--$δ$ measurements thereby become a sharp test: the predicted $δ$ sits $1.7σ$ from the present central value on the same profile likelihood, the predicted $\varepsilon_2$ has the opposite sign to the measured one, and the Hyper-Kamiokande design precision of $20^\circ$ on $δ$ resolves the question at $3σ$. The mass predictions are inherited unchanged, $Σm_ν=65.6$ meV and $m_{ββ}=5.4$ meV, at the current DESI bound in $Λ$CDM.

</details>


### [77] [Spectroscopic and Structural Properties of $B$, $B_s$, and $B_c$ Mesons within a Non-Relativistic Potential Model: A Comparative Analysis via Matrix Numerov and Variational Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08515)
*Pritom Kumar Nath,Rishan Dev Choudhury,Jugal Lahkar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study investigates low-energy properties of B mesons using a non-relativistic potential model, achieving high precision in mass predictions and a comparative analysis with other theoretical methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately determine the physical properties of B, B_s, and B_c mesons, addressing the limitations of previous models.

Method: The research utilizes the Killingbeck potential within a non-relativistic framework, employing both the matrix Numerov method and the quantum mechanical variational method to compute observables and fit parameters to the Cornell potential.

Result: The calculated masses of the mesons showed deviations of less than 0.5% from experimental data, with detailed discussions on the model's limitations and applicability.

Conclusion: The results are compared with other theoretical approaches, validating the effectiveness of the methods used and providing a comprehensive understanding of heavy meson physics.

Abstract: In this work, we studied the spectroscopic masses, decay constants, oscillation frequencies, and Isgur-Wise function parameters of $B$, $B_s$, and $B_c$ mesons within the framework of a non-relativistic potential model. The interaction is modelled using the Killingbeck potential, with calculations performed using two complementary approaches: the matrix Numerov method for solving the Schrödinger equation and the quantum mechanical variational method employing the Gaussian wave function. We determined the unknown parameters of the Killingbeck potential by fitting it to the Cornell potential using the ordinary least squares (OLS) method. These calibrated parameters are then used to compute the aforementioned physical observables.We obtained the masses of the heavy mesons with deviations of less than 0.5\% from the highly precise experimental data. We also discussed the drawbacks and limitations of the non-relativistic model and highlighted its domain of applicability. At the end, we compare our results obtained from both methods with those from other theoretical frameworks, including QCD sum rules, lattice QCD, and alternative potential model studies.

</details>


### [78] [Small-instanton effects in an atlas of KSVZ axion models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08602)
*Ning Chen,Saurabh K. Shukla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the impact of small-instanton contributions to the axion potential in KSVZ models with vector-like quarks, revealing how variations in quark configurations affect axion mass and couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how small-instanton contributions influence the axion potential in various KSVZ models and their implications for axion properties.

Method: The study uses naive dimensional analysis to examine contributions from single, multiple, and distinct vector-like quarks, ensuring perturbative gauge coupling behavior to the Planck scale and analyzing fermion zero-mode content.

Result: Increasing copies of vector-like quarks leads to dominance by small-size instantons, making the axion potential sensitive to UV completion, affecting mass and coupling characteristics.

Conclusion: This modified axion mass and coupling paradigm provides new regions in the $(m_a, g_{aγγ})$ plane for axion detection, suggesting alignment changes in standard relationships among axion properties.

Abstract: We investigate small-instanton contributions to the axion potential across a range of KSVZ models containing vector-like quarks~(VQs), using naive dimensional analysis. We consider scenarios containing a single VQ, multiple identical copies, and sets of distinct VQs, requiring in each case that the gauge couplings remain perturbative up to the Planck scale under the two-loop gauge running. The associated fermion zero-mode content varies between these cases, requiring different combinations of mass insertions and scalar--Yukawa contractions for its saturation. Increasing the copies of VQs can render the instanton-size integral dominated by instantons of the smallest size, corresponding to the scale near the ultraviolet~(UV) cut-off. The resulting contribution then becomes sensitive to the UV completion and the induced potential can compete with, or dominate over, the ordinary QCD contribution. Assuming that the QCD and small-instanton potentials are aligned, we determine the resulting axion-mass shift and its consequences for the axion--photon coupling. When the small-instanton induced susceptibility becomes comparable to or larger than the QCD susceptibility, the physical axion mass of $m_a$ is enhanced at fixed decay constant~$f_a$, while the axion-photon of $g_{aγγ}$ coupling remains controlled by $f_a$ and the anomaly ratio of $E/N$. The standard QCD relation among $m_a$, $f_a$ and $g_{aγγ}$ is consequently modified, opening new regions of the $(m_a,g_{aγγ})$ plane for axion searches.

</details>


### [79] [Phenomenology of different cross-section models at DUNE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08609)
*Subrajit Puhan,Monojit Ghosh,Rukmani Mohanta,Amit Pal,S. K. Swain*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study evaluates various cross-section models for neutrino oscillation parameters in DUNE, finding that the HF-CRPA+BS model outperforms the DUNE tune, especially for resonance interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the impact of different cross-section models on neutrino oscillation parameters in the DUNE experiment, which affects measurement accuracy.

Method: Comparative analysis of cross-section models including Llewellyn-Smith, Hartree-Fock CRPA, Rein-Sehgal, and Berger-Sehgal, with results benchmarked against the DUNE cross-section tune.

Result: The HF-CRPA+BS model shows significant improvements in total cross-section strength and measurements of neutrino parameters, outperforming the DUNE tuning model.

Conclusion: Utilizing the HF-CRPA+BS model can enhance estimates of neutrino mass ordering and other parameters by 15-30% compared to the existing DUNE framework.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the impact of different cross-section models in the measurement of the neutrino oscillation parameters in DUNE. In particular, for the quasi-elastic (QEL) region, we considered the Llewellyn-Smith formalism (LS) and the Hartree-Fock Continuum Random Phase Approximation (HF-CRPA) and for the resonance (RES) region, we consider the Rein-Sehgal (RS) and the Berger-Sehgal (BS) models, and compare our results with the DUNE cross-section tune (Valencia model for QEL and RS model for RES), which was considered in their technical design report. Our results show that while the DUNE tune is best for QEL, the best model for RES is BS. As the DUNE energy region is mainly dominated by RES, for the total cross-section, the HF-CRPA+BS model provides the best strength in the cross-section, whereas the DUNE tune is the weakest among all the configurations considered in our work. Regarding the neutrino mass ordering, CP violation and octant sensitivity, the HF-CRPA+BS model provides $(25- 30)$% improvement, and regarding the precision of the $θ_{23}$ and $Δm^2_{32}$, the same model provides $(15 - 20)$% improvement as compared to the DUNE tune.

</details>


### [80] [The role of thermal photons in a magnetized plasma and their significance in heavy ion collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08614)
*Zhaolu Ouyang,Xinyang Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study explores thermal photon production in a magnetized quark-gluon plasma, focusing on the impacts of magnetic fields and chemical properties on photon characteristics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the understanding of photon production in heavy-ion collisions, particularly addressing the photon $v_2$ puzzle and the role of magnetic fields.

Method: We use Landau-level quantization to analyze the effects of magnetic fields, chemical potential, and chiral chemical potential on thermal photon yield and polarization.

Result: The findings provide a more detailed theoretical description of photon production linked to heavy-ion collisions, which could help solve the photon $v_2$ puzzle and reveal magnetic field signatures.

Conclusion: This work paves the way for future investigations into magnetic fields and their effects in heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: In this contribution, we present thermal photon production mechanisms within a magnetized quark-gluon plasma, utilizing the framework of Landau-level quantization. We examine the specific influences of the magnetic field, chemical potential, and chiral chemical potential on photon yield and polarization. By providing a more comprehensive theoretical description of photon production across the full evolution of heavy-ion collisions, our study offers a promising new avenue for resolving the photon $v_2$ puzzle and potentially detecting the signature of magnetic fields in heavy-ion collision experiments.

</details>


### [81] [Sneutrino Tribrid Inflation in Flipped $\mathbf{SU(5)}$: Confronting ACT DR6 and Planck](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08708)
*Farishta Israr,Mansoor Ur Rehman,Saleh O. Allehabi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a model of sneutrino tribrid inflation within $R$-symmetric flipped $SU(5)$ GUT, exploring its implications for inflationary dynamics and baryogenesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The construction of a viable inflationary model that accommodates the flip symmetry and connects to baryogenesis mechanisms.

Method: The study uses a vector-like pair of matter multiplets to establish a $D$-flat inflaton direction, leveraging an independent superpotential cutoff scale to enlarge the parameter space, and analyzes it against observational data from ACT DR6 and Planck.

Result: Identifies viable parameters where the symmetry-breaking scale approaches conventional GUT scale while allowing for observable tensor-to-scalar ratios, and analyzes post-inflationary dynamics leading to baryon asymmetry through leptogenesis.

Conclusion: The model successfully links inflationary dynamics to baryogenesis within the constraints of existing observational data, offering a broader parameter space for future experiments.

Abstract: We construct a realization of sneutrino tribrid inflation within the $R$-symmetric flipped $SU(5)$ grand unified theory. A vector-like pair of matter multiplets, $10_V+\overline{10}_V$, provides a $D$-flat inflaton direction along the right-handed sneutrino component, with an additional $Z_2$ symmetry forbidding a direct inflaton mass term so that the leading inflationary interaction is a non-renormalizable Kähler-driven operator. Unlike previous studies, we treat the superpotential cutoff scale $M_s$ as independent of the reduced Planck mass, $m_P$, substantially enlarging the viable parameter space. Confronting the model with the ACT DR6/Planck determination $n_s=0.9734\pm0.0034$ and the $2σ$ bound on the running of the spectral index, we identify broad regions of viable parameter space in which the flipped $SU(5)$ symmetry-breaking scale can approach the conventional GUT scale, $M\simeq M_{\rm GUT}$, and the tensor-to-scalar ratio can reach observable values, $r\gtrsim10^{-3}$, within reach of forthcoming CMB $B$-mode experiments such as LiteBIRD and CMB-S4. We further study the post-inflationary dynamics, identifying the inflaton with the lightest right-handed sneutrino in a conventional type-I seesaw sector, whose out-of-equilibrium decay generates a lepton asymmetry that is converted into the observed baryon asymmetry via non-thermal leptogenesis and electroweak sphalerons, for a reheating temperature consistent with both successful leptogenesis and the gravitino constraint.

</details>


### [82] [Comment on "Regarding the Rotational Unruh Effect"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08759)
*S. R. Mane*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper critiques a document discussing the Rotational Unruh Effect, focusing on examples involving electrons in storage rings and their astrophysical relevance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address and critique claims made regarding the Rotational Unruh Effect in the context of high-energy electron behavior.

Method: Analysis of statements made in the referenced document and their implications for electron dynamics in various contexts.

Result: Insight into the applicability of the Rotational Unruh Effect to electron behavior in high-energy environments and astrophysical scenarios.

Conclusion: The discussion highlights significant examples and underscores the importance of this effect in both particle physics and astrophysics.

Abstract: We comment on various statements in a recent document (A.~Deur, S.~J.~Brodsky, C.~D.~Roberts and B.~Terzi{ć}, Regarding the Rotational Unruh Effect, \textit{arXiv:2607.25004v1 [hep.ph]}, (2026)). The examples cited treat electrons (or positrons) circulating and emitting photons in high-energy storage rings. The topic is also of interest in astrophysics, for electrons orbiting in magnetic fields around neutron stars.

</details>


### [83] [The Challenge of Detecting Quantum Nature of Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08803)
*Yu Miyauchi,Hidetoshi Omiya,Atsuhisa Ota,Hiroki Takeda,Takahiro Tanaka*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study examines the potential of squeezing as an observable signature of quantum gravitational waves, revealing that realistic detectors face limitations in detecting squeezing due to phase incoherence, solid angle coverage, and weak gravitational interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the observable effects of quantum gravitational waves and the role of squeezing in detection.

Method: Analyzing the effects of inflationary two-mode squeezing and investigating the interaction between squeezed detectors and classical gravitational fields.

Result: Squeezing can be observed with a quantized gravitational wave if the detector is initially in a squeezed state, but the signal is muted by weak graviton-detector coupling and other suppression factors.

Conclusion: Detector squeezing can be beneficial, but the challenges posed by weak interactions remain significant.

Abstract: We investigate whether squeezing can provide an observable signature of quantum gravitational waves. Because a realistic detector couples only to a particular wave-packet mode, squeezing in global source modes need not remain observable. We show that inflationary two-mode squeezing reduces to an unsqueezed thermal state in the accessible one-mode sector, phase incoherence washes out squeezing in stochastic backgrounds, and the limited coverage of the solid angle of detectors strongly suppresses squeezing from isolated sources. We then show that source squeezing is not essential, {\it i.e.}, a quantized gravitational wave can generate a positive squeezing witness if the detector state is initially prepared in a squeezed state, whereas a classical external gravitational field cannot, producing only a displacement. However, the resulting signal is bounded by the extremely small graviton--detector coupling. Thus, detector squeezing can remove the need for squeezed incident waves, but not the suppression caused by weak gravitational interaction.

</details>


### [84] [Improved Regge Kinematics and All-Path-Length Corrections to Momentum Broadening in the Quark-Gluon Plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08894)
*Dario van den Berg,Isobel Kolbe*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study enhances the GLV formalism for analyzing transverse momentum broadening of partons in QGP by incorporating APL corrections and sub-Regge kinematics, leading to significant modifications in transverse momentum distributions and transport coefficients, especially in small collision systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The standard GLV approach may not be reliable in small collision systems due to approximations that assume large separation distances and formation times.

Method: The study extends GLV to include APL corrections and improved Regge kinematics, deriving analytic expressions for transverse momentum broadening and momentum transport coefficients while relaxing standard approximations.

Result: The modified framework shows substantial changes in the transverse momentum broadening distribution and the transport coefficient $\,hat{q}$, especially for short path lengths and high exchanged momentum.

Conclusion: These enhancements provide a more accurate theoretical framework for understanding momentum broadening in quark-gluon plasma, addressing limitations in the previous GLV formalism.

Abstract: We present a study of transverse momentum broadening for high-energy partons propagating through the quark-gluon plasma (QGP), extending the Gyulassy-Levai-Vitev (GLV) formalism to include both all-path-length (APL) corrections and improved sub-Regge kinematics. The standard GLV framework relies on the large separation distance and large formation time approximations, which are well justified in large nuclei but may become unreliable in small collision systems, where the relevant length and coherence scales are comparable. Working to first order in the opacity expansion, we derive analytic expressions for the transverse momentum broadening distribution and the corresponding momentum transport coefficient, $\hat{q}$, while systematically relaxing these approximations. The APL correction arises from relaxing the large separation distance approximation, whereas the sub-Regge and combined sub-Regge-APL corrections are obtained using an improved set of Regge kinematics beyond the strict Regge limit. We find that these corrections can substantially modify both the transverse momentum broadening distribution and the momentum transport coefficient, particularly for short path lengths and high exchanged momentum, providing a more complete theoretical description of momentum broadening in the QGP.

</details>


### [85] [Quark spin-orbit correlations in spin-1 targets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09012)
*Hyunwoo Kim,June-Young Kim*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the quark spin-orbit correlation in hadrons, focusing on a QCD operator defining this correlation. It derives gauge-invariant sum rules relevant for spin-1 hadrons and estimates correlations for specific mesons and nuclei.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the relationship between quark helicity and orbital angular momentum in hadrons through spin-orbit correlations.

Method: The study employs QCD operators, analyzing the asymmetric parity-odd quark energy-momentum tensor and its decomposition into various tensor parts, leading to the derivation of two gauge-invariant sum rules for spin-orbit correlations.

Result: Two sum rules are established; one for unpolarized targets and another dependent on tensor polarization, with estimates for $ρ$ meson and deuteron spin-orbit correlations based on existing data.

Conclusion: The findings enhance the understanding of quark dynamics in hadrons, particularly for spin-1 targets, highlighting the unique aspects of spin-orbit correlation in different spin states.

Abstract: The quark spin-orbit correlation probes the alignment of quark helicity with longitudinal kinetic orbital angular momentum inside a hadron. This correlation is defined by a QCD operator: the position moment of the asymmetric parity-odd quark energy-momentum tensor. The matrix element of this rank-two tensor decomposes into symmetric-traceless, antisymmetric, and trace parts. The symmetric-traceless part is matched to moments of axial generalized parton distributions. The QCD equations of motion relate the antisymmetric part to vector and tensor form factors and set the trace to zero. Using these relations, we derive two gauge-invariant sum rules for the spin-orbit correlation in a spin-$1$ hadron. One gives the correlation in an unpolarized target. The other gives its tensor-polarization dependence, which is absent for spin-$0$ and spin-$1/2$ targets. We estimate the unpolarized spin-orbit correlations for the $ρ$ meson and deuteron using existing lattice and phenomenological inputs, respectively.

</details>


### [86] [Transverse distributions of the energy-momentum tensor for a spin-$3/2$ baryon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09242)
*Hui-Jae Lee,Ki-Hoon Hong,June-Young Kim,Hyun-Chul Kim*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a multipole framework for analyzing the energy-momentum tensor of a spin-$3/2$ baryon under longitudinal boosts, revealing key multipole contributions to energy and momentum distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to provide a comprehensive understanding of the energy-momentum tensor's structure for spin-$3/2$ baryons, particularly in relation to longitudinal boosts and their effects on multipole distributions.

Method: The authors develop a multipole representation for the energy-momentum tensor, establishing matrix elements in the transverse Breit frame and factorizing Lorentz mixing from Wigner rotations. They use light-front Rarita-Schwinger spinors to compute additional tensor components and highlight the relationship between transverse and infinite-momentum frames.

Result: The analysis reveals that the energy distribution is primarily governed by the energy monopole, remaining consistent under longitudinal boosts, while longitudinally polarized targets show unique monopole dominance and transversely polarized targets feature spin-dependent effects including quadrupole and octupole contributions.

Conclusion: This work provides crucial insights into the multipole structure of the energy-momentum tensor for spin-$3/2$ baryons, illustrating the impact of boosts on energy and momentum distributions and establishing a link between different reference frames.

Abstract: We develop a multipole description of transverse distributions of the energy-momentum tensor for a spin-$3/2$ baryon in frames connected by a longitudinal boost. In the transverse Breit frame, the $T^{00}$, $T^{03}$, and $T^{33}$ matrix elements are expressed through seven multipole form factors for energy, angular momentum, and stress. At finite longitudinal momentum, we factorize the Lorentz mixing of these three components from the spin-$3/2$ Wigner rotations of the external states. The resulting elastic-frame matrix elements contain six transverse multipoles, whose Fourier transforms define the distributions of energy, longitudinal momentum, and longitudinal momentum flux. We also calculate $T^{++}$, $T^{+-}$, and $T^{--}$ directly with light-front Rarita-Schwinger spinors. The elastic-frame construction provides a continuous interpolation from the transverse Breit frame to the infinite-momentum frame. In this limit, the Wigner rotation becomes the Melosh rotation, and the leading elastic-frame matrix elements reproduce the corresponding light-front results. Using the $Δ$-baryon gravitational form factors obtained in the Skyrme model as a representative numerical input, we find that the energy distribution is dominated by the energy monopole defined in the transverse Breit frame and changes only weakly under longitudinal boosts. Through boost mixing, this monopole provides the dominant contribution to the longitudinal momentum distribution and its flux at finite $P_z$. For a longitudinally polarized spin-$3/2$ target, the distributions contain only the monopole contributions, whereas those of a transversely polarized target exhibit spin-dependent quadrupole and octupole deformations and a dipole that shifts their maxima.

</details>


### [87] [Effects of Born-Infeld Electrodynamics on Chiral Symmetry Restoration and Meson Susceptibilities in Holographic QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09489)
*Hiwa A. Ahmed,Peshwaz A. Abdoul*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores chiral symmetry breaking in a holographic QCD model, identifying transitions at finite temperature and chemical potential through a nonlinear charged Born-Infeld black hole framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the implications of nonlinear bulk electrodynamics on chiral symmetry breaking and phase transitions in QCD at finite temperature and chemical potential.

Method: Numerical analysis of chiral symmetry breaking using a holographic QCD model based on a nonlinear charged Born-Infeld black hole background, extracting the chiral condensate as the order parameter.

Result: At zero chemical potential, a chiral crossover transition occurs at T_pc=0.1477 GeV for physical quark masses, becoming first-order at T_c=0.1337 GeV in the chiral limit. For finite chemical potential, the transition remains second-order with decreasing T_c as μ increases.

Conclusion: The study emphasizes the effects of nonlinear bulk electrodynamics on the chiral phase diagram and corroborates existing findings from soft-wall model studies.

Abstract: Within a holographic QCD framework, we numerically investigate chiral symmetry breaking and the associated phase transition at finite temperature and chemical potential. The model is constructed on a nonlinear charged Born-Infeld black hole background. The chiral condensate, extracted from the asymptotic behavior of the bulk scalar field, serves as the primary order parameter. At zero chemical potential, we find a chiral crossover transition for physical quark masses with a pseudocritical temperature of $T_{pc}=0.1477$ GeV. In the chiral limit, the transition becomes first-order with a critical temperature of $T_{c}=0.1337$ GeV. A critical strange quark mass of $m_s=37$ MeV, at zero light quark mass, separates first- and second-order transition regions. For finite chemical potential ($μ$) and a physical strange mass ($m_s=95$ MeV) with massless light quarks, the transition remains second-order, with $T_c$ decreasing as $μ$ increases. These results are further supported by the behavior of meson susceptibilities $(χ_π-χ_σ)$, which exhibit a rapid thermal decay and convergence across the phase boundary. Introducing the Born-Infeld parameter $β$ shifts the second-order phase boundary to higher temperatures for smaller $β$ (stabilizing the chirally broken phase) but does not alter the transition order or introduce a critical endpoint within the studied range. Our findings are consistent with previous soft-wall model studies and highlight the significant role of nonlinear bulk electrodynamics in modifying the chiral phase diagram.

</details>


### [88] [Prospects for early heavy-quark measurements at the EIC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09608)
*Allencris John Rubesh Rajan,Laboni Manna*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper evaluates the promising aspects of heavy-quark-pair electroproduction in $ep$ and $e$A collisions at the early phase of the EIC, estimating relevant cross sections, distributions, and event yields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a comprehensive understanding of heavy-quark-pair electroproduction processes and their feasibility with the upcoming EIC data, improving upon earlier HERA results.

Method: The study estimates total cross sections and differential distributions for various parameters and evaluates different Bjorken-$x$ intervals, using anticipated EIC luminosities and beam energies.

Result: Enhanced event yields and statistical precision over HERA measurements for moderate and large $x_{m Bj}$; computed nuclear modification ratios $R^{e	ext{A}}$ highlighting shadowing and suppression effects.

Conclusion: The findings establish a parton-level baseline for appraising early EIC data and sets the stage for advanced theoretical and experimental research.

Abstract: We assess the prospects for inclusive heavy-quark-pair electroproduction in electron-proton ($ep$) and electron-nucleus ($e$A) collisions, alongside double-charm-pair ($D^0 \bar{D}^0$ and $D^0D^0$) electroproduction in $ep$ collisions, during the early operational phase of the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC). Based on the anticipated beam energies and luminosities of the EIC, we estimate the total cross sections and differential distributions for photon virtuality $Q^2$, heavy-quark transverse momentum, and rapidity. This analysis evaluates different Bjorken-$x$ ($x_{\rm Bj}$) intervals across a range of $Q^2$ values. Expected event yields are estimated from the projected integrated luminosities of the early science programme, demonstrating the substantial improvement in statistical precision over previous HERA measurements at moderate and large $x_{\rm Bj}$. For electron--nucleus collisions, we compute the nuclear modification ratio $R^{e\text{A}}$ within collinear factorisation using nuclear parton distribution functions, and identify the kinematic regimes in which shadowing, anti-shadowing, and EMC-like suppression are accessible with early EIC data. These results are intended to serve as a parton-level baseline to support feasibility studies and to provide a reference for future higher-order theoretical and experimental investigations at the EIC.

</details>


### [89] [NNLO soft functions for heavy-quark pair production at hadron colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09621)
*Guido Bell,Alessandro Broggio,Sebastian Edelmann,Matthew A. Lim,Rudi Rahn*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper extends the SoftSERVE framework to compute NNLO soft functions for heavy quarks in back-to-back kinematics, presenting new results for the 0-jettiness soft function in top-quark pair production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide accurate calculations of soft functions for processes involving heavy quarks at higher orders in perturbative QCD, enhancing the precision of theoretical predictions in particle physics.

Method: Utilizing the SoftSERVE framework, the authors develop an algorithm with phase-space parameterisations for massive and mixed contributions, focusing on SCET$_	ext{I}$ observables and performing detailed renormalisation.

Result: Explicit results for the 0-jettiness soft function are presented as two-dimensional grids, showcasing the behavior of tripole color correlations in the threshold limit.

Conclusion: The study contributes to the understanding of soft functions in high-energy physics, paving the way for more accurate predictions in top-quark processes.

Abstract: We generalise the SoftSERVE framework for the automated computation of soft functions involving final-state heavy quarks in back-to-back kinematics up to next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) in the strong-coupling expansion. Our algorithm employs suitable phase-space parameterisations for both massive and mixed massless-massive contributions, allowing for an efficient numerical evaluation. Focusing on SCET$_\mathrm{I}$ observables that satisfy non-Abelian exponentiation, we present explicit results for the 0-jettiness soft function in hadronic top-quark pair production in the form of two-dimensional grids. We describe the renormalisation procedure in detail and discuss the singular behaviour of the tripole colour correlations in the threshold limit.

</details>


### [90] [Leptogenesis in Brane-modified cosmology: Signatures in primordial gravitational waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09814)
*Amit Dutta Banik,Arnab Paul*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study explores how brane-inspired modifications in the early universe's evolution affect baryogenesis via leptogenesis, highlighting new regions for successful leptogenesis and its observable implications through gravitational waves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the effects of altered cosmic histories on baryogenesis and leptogenesis, expanding the parameter space for potential success in leptogenesis.

Method: The authors analyze scenarios with single and multiple epochs of stiff equations of state post-inflation and pre-radiation domination, modifying the Boltzmann equations that govern lepton asymmetry.

Result: Successful leptogenesis is shown to be feasible in parameter regions not accessible under standard cosmology. Additionally, modifications impact the spectral energy density of primordial gravitational waves, which may provide observational insights.

Conclusion: This work offers a novel indirect method to probe high-scale leptogenesis scenarios through the expected observable characteristics of gravitational waves.

Abstract: We investigate the implications of brane-inspired modifications of the evolutionary history of the early universe on the process of baryogenesis via leptogenesis. A modified cosmic history alters the evolution of the Boltzmann equations governing lepton asymmetry, providing the possibility of successful leptogenesis in regions of the parameter space inaccessible in the standard cosmological scenario. Furthermore, a modified cosmic history also alters the shape of an otherwise scale-invariant spectral energy density (SED) of primary gravitational waves (PGWs) produced during inflation. This establishes a novel yet indirect probe of high scale leptogenesis scenarios through the observation of the SED of PGWs via future observations. We consider two scenarios in this work with single and multiple epochs of stiff equation of state(s) after inflation and before the epoch of radiation domination. We identify the parameter space where successful leptogenesis is possible, along with the possible observability of the PGWs, hence providing an indirect window into this leptogenesis scenario through PGWs.

</details>


<div id='quant-ph'></div>

# quant-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [91] [Beyond the Quantum Promise: A Security Analysis of Classical Control in Quantum Key Distribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07626)
*Ali Hamza Malik,Raja Hasnain Anwar,Muhammad Taqi Raza*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a formal verification framework for Quantum Key Distribution protocols that highlights vulnerabilities due to classical post-processing interactions and proposes improvements to enhance security.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the underexplored security implications of cross-layer interactions in QKD protocols, which rely on both quantum and classical components.

Method: The authors developed a symbolic model in Tamarin that automates the analysis of QKD protocols, assessing how classical operations impact quantum security guarantees.

Result: The framework identified three vulnerabilities in existing protocols and confirmed that two proposed enhancements eliminate these issues.

Conclusion: The study suggests systematic integration of classical and quantum components in QKD protocols and has conveyed findings to standardization bodies for further action.

Abstract: Quantum Key Distribution (QKD) protocols provide information-theoretic security by using quantum mechanical principles. Yet QKD is fundamentally a hybrid protocol: its security depends on the correct integration of the quantum phase with classical post-processing. While ETSI and ITUT specifications standardize QKD architectures and interfaces, they evaluate protocol security in isolation, leaving cross-layer interactions as an underexplored attack surface. This paper introduces a formal verification framework that holistically models QKD protocols based on ETSI and ITUT QKD specifications. Our model is the first hybrid QKD protocol model that supports automated analysis of protocollevel security focusing on how classical operations influence the security guarantees provided by the quantum phase of the QKD protocol. We formalize a comprehensive symbolic model of QKD protocols, based on ETSI and ITU-T QKD specifications, in Tamarin, an automated protocol verifier. Applying this framework, we obtain formal evidence of three specification-level vulnerabilities in ETSI- and ITU-T-grounded protocol models under adversary Eve+: subverted entanglement injection, basis-deferred measurement, and message reflection. Each arises from a classical control-plane omission in the procedure text and is established under a symbolic abstraction rather than as a claim about all practical deployments. We introduce two protocol improvements: measurement commitment and identitybound message authentication codes (MACs). Tamarin verification confirms that these countermeasures eliminate the identified vulnerabilities under Eve+. We have communicated our results and recommendations to relevant standardization organizations.

</details>


### [92] [Readout-Rank Laws for Isotropic Quantum Tangents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07628)
*Marwan Ait Haddou*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The study explores the disparity between parameter sensitivity in quantum circuits and the minimal response of observables, focusing on fixed measurements. It establishes that information fractions behave as independent Beta variables. Experimental results across multiple circuit families align with theoretical predictions, highlighting the importance of tangent isotropy in understanding quantum information dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Investigate the mismatch between parameter changes in quantum circuits and the observables' responses to understand information retention in quantum systems better.

Method: The study compares quantum Fisher information with Fisher information across various quantum circuit setups, utilizing Haar random states to derive information fraction distributions and conducting exact-statevector experiments.

Result: Proved two successive information fractions are independent Beta variables contributing to the understanding of information retention, with empirical results showing increasing agreement between theoretical predictions and experimental outcomes.

Conclusion: Tangent isotropy is crucial in accurately predicting behavior in quantum circuits, as seen in the differing results between conserving and non-conserving ensembles.

Abstract: Deep parameterized quantum circuits may remain sensitive to a parameter change while the observables retained by a learning model barely respond. We study this separation for a fixed computational-basis measurement. For a pure-state tangent, we compare the quantum Fisher information $F_Q$, the Fisher information $F_{\rm full}$ in the complete bitstring distribution, and the largest variance-normalized response $\mathcal I_{\mathcal A}$ available to a diagonal readout space $\mathcal A$. If the joint state--tangent frame is Haar random, we prove that the two successive information fractions are independent Beta variables whose means are $1/2$ and $r/(2^n-1)$, where $r$ is the centered dimension of the readout. Consequently, even the joint span of all computational-basis Pauli strings through any fixed weight $k$ retain only $O(n^k2^{-n})$ of the full-record information. Exact-statevector experiments across six circuit families show increasing finite-size agreement with this hierarchy in five nonconserving ensembles as the circuit depth grows. A number-conserving family departs strongly from the isotropic prediction even after correcting the support and readout rank, showing that rank alone is insufficient without tangent isotropy.

</details>


### [93] [Storage, Scrambling, and Loss of Information in the Quantum Reservoir Computing Paradigm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07677)
*Nathan Keenan,Roberta Zambrini*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the use of a quantum reservoir computing (QRC) framework for time-series processing, examining the dynamical properties of the system, mutual information metrics, and nonlocal information scrambling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess how the dynamical properties of QRC platforms influence their ability to process time-series data effectively.

Method: Introduces a classical-quantum state from the process tensor and uses Holevo quantities to analyze information saturation, memory fading, and the accessibility of input data for QRC tasks.

Result: Identifies diagnostics for nonlocal scrambling of information and quantifies information loss in relation to QRC performance across different parameters.

Conclusion: Suggests new avenues for research in QRC, based on the findings related to information dynamics and practical performance assessments.

Abstract: The suitability of a quantum reservoir computing (QRC) platform for a given time-series processing task is closely tied to the dynamical properties of its computational substrate and design. Information is injected into, processed by, and read from this substrate, and finally passed to a linear readout layer which is trained to perform a specific task. In this work we introduce a classical-quantum state derived from the process tensor representing the dynamical part of this process for typical QRC protocols found in the literature. Using this object, mutual informations between physical subsystems and subsets of past inputs can be written as Holevo quantities, which we then use to numerically investigate information saturation in the substrate, fading memory of past inputs, and the local accessibility of injected information for a commonly used QRC platform. We then extract two diagnostics that characterise the nonlocal scrambling of information within, and loss of information from the substrate, and compare these to QRC performance across Hamiltonian parameters and measurement strengths. Finally, we comment on future directions that the framework introduced here opens up for the study and extension of the QRC program.

</details>


### [94] [Emergent Problem-Graph Alignment in RL-Discovered Entanglement Topologies for QAOA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07686)
*Tobias Rohe,Federico Harjes Ruiloba,Markus Baumann,Gerhard Stenzel,Leo Sünkel,Thomas Gabor,Claudia Linnhoff-Popien*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: This study explores using reinforcement learning to identify more effective entanglement topologies for the Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm (QAOA) in MaxCut optimization, outperforming standard methods under limited optimization budgets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether non-standard entanglement topologies can improve QAOA performance without direct problem graph knowledge, addressing limitations of traditional coupling to problem structure.

Method: A Masked Proximal Policy Optimization agent constructs circuit topologies by sequentially placing IsingZZ gates, using a variational loop to optimize QAOA parameters based on sparse rewards related to approximation ratios.

Result: In tests on Erdős–Rényi instances with up to 10 qubits, the agent discovered topologies that substantially overlapped with problem graphs, outperforming conventional methods under limited optimization but lagging behind in dense topology scenarios.

Conclusion: The findings indicate that topology density influences the trade-off between trainability and expressibility, with underlying structural cues encoded in the variational optimization landscape.

Abstract: In the Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm (QAOA), the entanglement topology, where qubit pairs are connected by two-qubit gates, is conventionally set equal to the edge set of the problem graph. This coupling ties circuit design to explicit problem knowledge and may not yield the most trainable circuit under limited optimization budgets. We investigate whether a reinforcement learning (RL) agent can discover more effective entanglement topologies for QAOA-based MaxCut optimization without direct access to the problem graph. A Masked Proximal Policy Optimization agent sequentially places IsingZZ gates to construct a circuit topology, while a variational inner loop optimizes the resulting QAOA parameters and returns the approximation ratio as a sparse terminal reward. The agent's observation contains only the edges placed so far and the current approximation ratio; graph structure can only be inferred indirectly through the optimization reward. On Erdős--Rényi instances with up to $10$~qubits, the agent consistently converges to topologies that are strict subsets of the problem graph, achieving overlap ratios approaching $1.0$, despite receiving no explicit information about the graph structure in its observations. These sparse, problem-aligned topologies outperform the full graph topology and several structural baselines when the optimization budget is limited ($50$~gradient steps), but are overtaken by denser topologies given sufficient optimization budget. Our results reveal a trainability--expressibility trade-off governed by topology density and suggest that the variational optimization landscape implicitly encodes structural information about the problem Hamiltonian.

</details>


### [95] [State Preparation Protocols for Entangled States via Open Quantum Walks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07695)
*Pedro Linck,Nadja K. Bernardes*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The paper explores open quantum walks (OQWs) for quantum state preparation using nonunitary operators, specifically focusing on Dicke and GHZ states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop protocols for quantum state preparation that leverage the unique properties of open quantum walks and nonunitary operations.

Method: The authors construct a ring-shaped OQW to prepare Dicke states and develop a two-node protocol for GHZ states using Kraus operators derived from projective measurements.

Result: The OQW can prepare ensembles of Dicke states, with convergence dependent on the spectral gap of the Markov chain, while the GHZ state protocol prepares states deterministically without measuring the walker.

Conclusion: The quantum trajectories method integrates smoothly into the OQW framework, enhancing its application for quantum state preparation.

Abstract: Open quantum walks couple transitions on a graph to quantum operations on an internal degree of freedom. We use this structure to formulate protocols for quantum state preparation with nonunitary Kraus operators. We construct a ring-shaped OQW preparing an ensemble of Dicke states, with W states appearing as the single-excitation case, from which any individual Dicke state is recovered by postselecting the walker position; its convergence is governed by the spectral gap of the underlying Markov chain, for which we obtain a closed-form approximate expression. For GHZ states we present a two-node protocol using Kraus operators built from projective measurements that prepares them deterministically without requiring a measurement of the walker, and analyze how unsharp measurements affect the results and the convergence of the walk. We also show that the quantum trajectories method embeds naturally in the OQW framework as a graph-structured collision model.

</details>


### [96] [Preparing approximate $N$-fold cat states with the phase space instruction set](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07696)
*Shuyan Zhou,Andrew Lucas*

Main category: quant-ph

TL;DR: The study identifies the inefficiency of the phase space instruction set in preparing large Schrödinger cat states, requiring significant circuit depth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the efficiency of the phase space instruction set for preparing complex quantum states and to analyze circuit depth requirements.

Method: The authors demonstrate the necessary circuit depth using this gate set and derive a protocol for prime numbers N that achieves the asymptotic bound.

Result: A circuit depth of Ω(ϕ(N)) is necessary for state preparation, showing inefficiency in the universal gate set, particularly for prime N.
